## ⛭ OPENING A TURN — the three that fire the operating layer *(added r1702; the list had none of them)*
*The manual, the wisdom ledger and the mirror-check were all **reachable** — the README routes to the manual at
step 8b, `INDEX` catalogues the ledger — and **nothing named them at the moment of use**. That is the inert-rule
problem one level out: `THE_INTERFERENCE_ENGINE` §4, **"a rule behind a door the disposition keeps shut is
inert."** These three open the door.*

1. **Run the mirror-check, and make it visible in the reply — the METHOD is §The mirror below; this is the
   trigger.** *Before going at the problem, name the **pair**
   most at risk for **this turn's specific work** — not a recitation, one pair chosen for the work in front of
   you — and decide not to fall into it. **Pull the apt pair, not one face** (`CODA_FIELD_NOTE` r659: almost
   every face is one pole of over-reach ↔ under-reach, so a single-face mirror catches half a step).*
   **Never skip it on a turn that produces a verdict; the verdict is exactly when it is needed.**
2. **Open `THE_WISDOM_LEDGER.md` at the compartment for what you are about to do** — *it is filed **by moment of
   use**, not by topic: A at spin-up · B before opening a paper · C while reading · D before claiming · E before
   editing · F before writing a receipt · G when quoting a value · H when propagating · I when closing the turn ·
   J when placing a scrap.* **A ledger filed by moment of use and never opened at that moment is a catalogue.**
3. **Feed it back.** *A scrap earned this turn is filed this turn, in the compartment where it will fire — and
   when a pass touches the ledger, run the **condition sweep** at its head over the four conditional discharges.*

**And `THE_OPERATING_MANUAL.md` is the method for this layer** — read at spin-up (README step 8b), then consulted
the way `THE_ARSENAL` is consulted for a paper: before a load-bearing **decision** (the operable question), before
writing a receipt's **verdict** (read the output first), and at the **pause** (if a thing wants a fresh turn, say
so and stop).

## ⌗ AND THE OTHER PER-TURN LAYER — `THE_ARSENAL`'s five ground tools *(cross-pointed r1749; neither list named the other)*
**`THE_ARSENAL` LEVEL 3 says its five disciplines are "carried every turn, every paper" — and this list carried
none of four of them.** *Two per-turn layers, neither pointing at the other: SA-2's fourth shape, **two right
things in their right places that do not know about each other.** Not merged — **this list is the turn's
mechanics and the corpus-wide rules; those five are what you carry INTO a paper** — but a node running one and
not the other is running half.*
**The five, with where each is defined:**
- **The anti-flinch discipline** — `THE_ARSENAL` §The anti-flinch discipline. *The failure the project exists to
  defeat: **finding a defect, then deferring to the corpus's own statement about itself instead of fixing it** —
  inheriting a hedge, a stale masthead, a self-description as though it were authoritative. **The tell is that
  the correction gets handed back to Daryl as a "judgment call" instead of made.***
- **The do-not-assert census** — `THE_ARSENAL` §★ (Daryl, r1226).
- **The completion-shield** — `THE_ARSENAL` §★ (Daryl, r1230). *Every claim of incompleteness must carry, right
  there, the logical reason it is genuinely incomplete: a named wall, a named missing construction, or an honest
  empirical gap. **An assertion of incompleteness with no reason attached is the flinch turned self-sabotaging.***
- **The closure self-check** — **⚠ my r1749 note that it is "defined nowhere" was WRONG, corrected r1750. It is
  defined at the head of `THE_CLOSURE_LEDGER.md`**, the document whose name is the word: *"**Is this a closure?**
  → **treat-as-yes**, explore → what's filled / what's not → unfilled **worked now if possible, else
  surfaced-and-handed-over** → **close only when all parts filled, at honestly-earned weight.**"* With four
  verdicts: **OPENED** (reversed a premature close) · **WORKED-SHUT** (earned, all pieces present) ·
  **SURFACED** (genuine open handed over) · **CODA-FIX** (a catch that fed a coda face or rule).
  *I searched this document and `THE_ARSENAL` and concluded absence from a bounded search — the tenth instance
  of that class in the programme's record.*
- **The stale-link prior** — *no section of its own in the arsenal; its content is in this list's `Every turn`
  and metadocument checks, so it is covered under another name.*
**⌗ Direction of the pointer both ways:** *the arsenal's LEVEL 3 now says the turn mechanics live here.*

## ⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔ AND VALIDATE THE WHOLE RANGE BEFORE COMPARING, NOT THE PART YOU NEED (Daryl, r2003)

***"So you want to run the comparison BEFORE validating across the entire range? Every time we run such a
comparison we find that we needed to validate more of it."***

**⌗ THE RECORD IS UNAMBIGUOUS.** *Four times in this sector I have declared a comparison ready and been
wrong:*
*• `M4` — ran the Planck likelihood on a hybrid, and the hybrid could not be repaired;
• `A.40` — "the control passes", tested only $\ell\ge120$, and the plateau was 3× out;
• `A.63` — "validated on $110<\ell<860$", and the ends were both truncations of mine;
• `A.66` — "validated across the acoustic range", with $\ell=10$ overshooting at 1.48.*

**⌗ AND THE TEMPTATION IS ALWAYS THE SAME SHAPE:** *"the range I have validated is the range the comparison
needs." **It has never once been true**, because the ranges are not independent — a missing term at low $\ell$
is a missing term in the source, and the source feeds every multipole through the Bessel tails.*

> ***THE RULE: validate the whole observable range against the reference before running any comparison on
> part of it. "Good enough where it matters" is a judgement about a model you have just demonstrated you do
> not fully understand.***

**⌗ AND DARYL'S SECOND HALF MATTERS TOO, so it is recorded with the first:** *every premature comparison
**did** inform how to validate — `A.40` taught that a control validates the range it samples, `A.63` produced
the curve-of-agreement method, `A.66` showed that structure beats tuning. ***The attempts were not wasted;
they were how the validation method got built. The error is in what was CLAIMED from them, not in having run
them.***

## ⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔ RUN THE CONTROL BEFORE READING A NUMBER AS A PREDICTION (Daryl, r1960)

***"Everything isn't a percent or so off here and there and fitting everything to incredible precision and
then off by 50% on one calculation, followed by 'I might have just toppled the whole thing with this one
number that's WAY off while everything else fits beautifully.'"***

**⌗ WHAT HAPPENED.** *Four attempts at the CMB peak heights produced ratios of 19, then 8.9, against a
measured 2.21 — and I twice wrote them up as possibly-real predictions with a mechanism attached. **The same
model run on ΛCDM gives 3.22 where the measurement is 2.21, and 12.0 where it is 2.26.** The model fails on
the reference cosmology just as hard. There was no prediction.*

**⌗ THE DIAGNOSTIC, which is Daryl's and is general:**
> ***In a calculation where everything else agrees to a percent, a single result off by a FACTOR is a fault in
> that result. It is never the first place to look for a discovery.***

**⌗ THE RULE:**
> ***Before any number from a model is read as a claim about the framework, run that model on the reference
> cosmology and check it reproduces what is measured there. If it does not, the model is the finding.***

*It cost one function call. Four attempts went by without it.*

## ⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔ AND THE STANDING REFLEX: I ASK RIGHTWARD QUESTIONS OF A LEFTWARD CONSTRUCTION (Daryl, r1939)

***"The rates and everything are just set."***

**⌗ THREE INSTANCES IN THREE TURNS, and they are one error:**
*• **r1937** — read P6 as **forbidding** hypersurface-orthogonality when it forbids **privileging** it;
• **r1937** — jumped from "CAMB cannot express it" to "rewrite the Fortran", an engineering answer to a physics
question I had not asked;
• **r1939** — asked **which ruling the rate is read on**, when the two are coequal opposites, exchanged by $R$,
alternating across the recursion — and **the rate is SET by the geometry**.*

**⌗ THE DIAGNOSIS.** ***Every one takes a construction that reads leftward — the cut is primary, $
ho$ is the
name of its bend — and asks it a rightward question: what sources the rate, what may be added to or removed
from the sum, which component decides.*** **In the leftward reading there is no sum to add to. The rate is
set; the decomposition into components is bookkeeping performed afterwards.**

**⌗ THE TEST, before asking anything of this construction:**
> ***Does my question presuppose that content determines geometry? If so it is malformed here, and no answer
> to it will be found — its absence is not a missing mechanism.***

**⌗ AND THE CONSEQUENCE FOR TOOLING.** *A Boltzmann code assembles $H$ from a species list. **That is not a
missing feature to be patched — it is what the tool is.** A computation on this construction starts from the
rate the geometry sets and reads the content off it, which is exactly what `C8`/`C9`/`C10` did: each reduced
to an integral over the rate with the microphysics outside it, and none needed a code.*

## ⛔⛔⛔⛔⛔ AND AN OLD RECEIPT'S PROSE IS NOT A RESULT — IT IS AN ARTEFACT TO BE AUDITED (Daryl, r1923)

***"Do not be fooled by notes left around randomly by nodes who were running other computations that were
blowing up on them, probably because they were doing it all wrong. You can only use these things on the
condition you don't trust them to have been done right."***

**⌗ WHAT I DID.** *Having derived the driving envelope from first principles, I reached for
`damping_reabsorption.py`'s **prose "anti-panic audit"** to frame the result — quoted its conditional at
length, declared I had closed it, and reported a multi-sigma tension. **The file crashes on import:
`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'camb'`.*** *I also imported $\ell_D=1400$, the damping form, and the
$+9\%$ — none of it derived, all of it from the CAMB-retrofit era.*

**⌗ AND IT IS THE CORPUS'S OWN NAMED TRAP WITH THE HALVES SWAPPED.** *`full_transfer_verdict.py` imported
ΛCDM's **peaks** and applied CR's **damping**. **I derived CR's peaks and imported ΛCDM's damping
machinery.*** ***The rule the trap encodes is not "don't import peaks" — it is: **both halves must come
from the same place, and that place must be this construction.***

**⌗ THE RULE:**
> ***A receipt's CODE is evidence, and only if it runs. A receipt's PROSE is a note by a node under
> pressure, and it is an artefact to be audited like any other — never a framing to adopt.***
> **And a number lifted from a file that will not execute is not a number: it is a rumour.**

**⌗ THE TEST, cheap and it would have caught this:** *before using anything from a receipt, **run it**. If it
does not run, its conclusions are unverified by construction, whatever their tone.*

## ⛔⛔⛔ AND NOTHING GOES UNRESOLVED WITHOUT COMING TO DARYL FIRST (Daryl, r1906)

***"I want things collected and then presented as the option before moving on. ALWAYS. If it doesn't come to me

**⌗ THE r1900 RULE WAS HALF THE FIX AND I TREATED IT AS THE WHOLE ONE.** *r1900 said: settle it, or it does not
enter the corpus. **I then started routing unsettled things into field ledgers and continuing** — which keeps the
papers clean and still leaves the thing languishing, now somewhere Daryl is not reading.*

**⌗ THE COMPLETE RULE, and it has three branches, not two:**
> ***① SETTLE IT in the same turn — always the first attempt.***
> ***② IF IT WILL NOT SETTLE: COLLECT IT, and PRESENT IT TO DARYL AS THE OPTION BEFORE MOVING ON. Do not
> continue to the next thing. The turn ends on the collected list.***
> ***③ ONLY IF HE DEFERS IT does it go into the plan as a placed item, with its shape and its cost.***

**A field ledger is not a destination for an unresolved thing. It is a working file. An item that goes there and
is not surfaced has been abandoned in a tidier place.**

**⌗ AND THE TELL I KEEP MISSING:** *the phrase "recorded as work" or "held in the ledger" **followed by a
sentence proposing the next move** is the failure, every time. **If a turn produces something unresolved, the
turn's last words are the collected list — not a suggestion.***

**⌗ AND STALE QUEUE ENTRIES ARE THE SAME DISEASE, one revision later.** *The r1906 sweep found two in the
complex-analysis queue: one resting on `X1`'s **withdrawn** thrice-punctured sphere, one **already closed at
r1888** and still listed as open. **Both had outlived their own results by twenty revisions.** When a result is
withdrawn or a queue item closed, the queue is corrected in the same turn.*

> ***A probe that turns up a question either SETTLES it in the same turn, or the question does not enter the

***⛭ AND TWO ITEMS PLACED r1920, from the Phase-7 driving build (Daryl).***

**② ⟐ THE QUARK/LEPTON QUESTION — LOGGED FOR AFTER THE DRIVING BUILD.** *Home: **`THE_PHYSICS_REACH` ⑥b** (placed r1921; it is a physics item and was briefly misfiled in R-M).* ***`lepton` ×1 in the whole corpus
and that one is inside a bibliography title; `quark` ×0*** — *against `fermion` ×153, `generation` ×167,

and never names either species in its own voice.** *And **`baryon` is used 100% cosmologically*** — 23 of 26
as the $\eta$ datum — ***so the corpus has no particle-level account of what a baryon IS.***
**⌗ DARYL'S READING, and it sets the item's shape: working the quark/lepton split should also identify the
baryons at the particle level, and the P16 writing above is related to it and worth holding while the

**⌫ NOT A BLOCKER, and the reasoning is on record:** *the driving build needs exactly one number from the
neutrino sector, $f_\nu$, and BBN fixes it.*

***✔ AND THE ONE ITEM PLACED AT r1899 WAS SETTLED AT r1900, NOT LEFT.*** *The dimension-seven stratum: **EMPTY, and by P9's own bound.** A swept geometry inherits a subgroup of $\mathfrak{so}(4,1)$, and neither dim-7 symmetric pair of $\mathfrak{so}(5,1)$ embeds there — $\mathfrak{so}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(1,1)$ because $\mathfrak{so}(4)$ already uses all four spacelike directions and a boost on any of them will not commute; $\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(2)$ because one spacelike direction is left and a rotation needs two. **So $\{6,7,10\}$ is about the algebra and "exactly two strata" is about the reachable class, and they are consistent.** *P12 corrected: the dangling question is replaced by the answer.*

***"Let's stick to the super intriguing ones while they're still coming out of the woodwork."***

**① THE LIVE LEADS, while they are live.** *Probes whose neighbours have just paid — **projective duality
first**, since `Q6r` closed `A18` by polarity and the quadric queue records the corpus's hyperplane duals as
unexamined.* **A lead is worth most in the turn after the thing next to it worked.**
**② THE CLOSURES.** *Things partially seen through: the conformal field's **special conformal transformations**
("the one place it could still bite"), the **other reality-line crossings** (`X3r` — a one-line check), the
**Klein correspondence**, `A5.3`.*
**③ PHASE 7 — the build.** *Its gate is **over-information, not completion** — the build starts when 8 has given it enough that it is not reaching for whatever comes to hand. **Seven audited fields have now fed it.** The gate assesses whether that condition is met and acts on the assessment.*
**④ THE REMAINING REACH STATIONS — LAST.** *R-M's Ⓕ, Ⓖ, Ⓗ; R-P's ③④ onward *(**RECONCILED r2376+c54.32, `L-38`**: the R-P table's "①② QM is the earliest unrun station" and this file's "③④ onward" are **not in conflict** — ①② were run and never marked struck, so the earliest station **unmarked** is ①② and the earliest **unrun** is ③④. The table is stale, this file is right, and the fix is a strike not a rerun.)*.* ***And with a precondition this
audit earned: TWO stations turned out to be already held in the papers (Ⓑ in `prop:triple`'s proof, Ⓓ in
`rem:galois`). Check the papers before opening any of them.***

## ★★★ AND WHEN A RESULT IS A JOINT, INDEX IT ON EVERY FACE — `scripts/check_multiface.sh` (Daryl, r1883)

***The corpus's characteristic move is "one X worn N ways" — thirty such statements across nine papers. Each is
a joint. And a joint gets indexed under whichever face the indexer came from.***

*P5 states **"one $S_3$ worn three ways: monodromy, Weyl, Galois."** §0's $\sigma$ row carried two of the three;
§1i — the P5 card, fifteen thousand characters — carried `monodromy` ×8, `deck` ×8, **`Galois` ×0.** A node
approaching from the Galois face could not see that the corpus already held it, **and did not.***

**⌗ THE CHECK, two parts:** *① **does the declared count match the items enumerated?*** *(p0's masthead said SIX
while its section enumerated SEVEN — and the masthead's own altitude line already said seven.)* ② ***does every
index carrying the result carry EVERY face?***

**⌗ AND WHEN LANDING A NEW FACE ON AN EXISTING JOINT: update the count, and update every index that carries it.**
*The seventh face was added to p0's section and the masthead's count was not, which is how the drift starts.*

## ★★★ GREP TO FIND, READ TO CONFIRM — the four confirmations, before any edit that lands a claim (Daryl, r1874)

***"Interpretation can draw you to do really wrong things on grep… we still have to be sure we are confirming
what we are doing is right and is not deviating from the corpus, which is as coherent as we've been able to see,
and do NOT want it drifting in that regard through sloppy analysis."***

**The grep locates; it never adjudicates.** *Keep the speed — the sweeps, counts and cross-references are how the
programme moves. **Add the confirmations.***

**① ONTOLOGY** — *name the **rung** (`ONTOLOGY_FOUNDATION_INDEX` §0's five) and the **level** (§1·LEVELS: L1 the
existent's own rate · L2 the leaf's dynamics · L3 the projection). **If the claim cannot be stated in those
words it is not understood well enough to land.***
**② SECTION** — *read **the whole section it lands in**, at source, heading to heading. **Not the sentence the
grep returned.** Three Phase-8 claims re-derived results whose own proofs sat a paragraph away.*
**③ APPARATUS** — *the one swing and its three projections; hinge / manifold observer / charting observer; the
bounded dial; the crest as branch point; the two cubics. ***Ask: which window am I reading, and what is it blind
to?*** P3 §sec:params names each blindness, so the coupled complement is never optional.*
**④ COMPUTATION** — ***state the object, its base, its parametrisation, and the corpus's own dial, before
running anything.*** *`X1` failed exactly here: Riemann–Hurwitz needs a compact base, P5 works on a plane, and
the compactification was silent. **A computation on a base the construction does not live on returns real
theorems about the wrong object.***

***AND THE STANDING TEST: before landing, say in one sentence what this changes about how the corpus hangs
together. "Nothing, it is an addition beside" is a reason to ask again. Naming a joint is a reason to land.***

*Full form and the failure that established it: `PHASE8_DIFF_AUDIT.md` §4.*

## ★★★ WRITING ABOUT THE GEOMETRY — name the rung. **Every time, in every document, new or old.**

**⛭ AND EVERY FIELD BAKE CLOSES WITH FOUR THINGS** *(r1862, r1865 — Daryl):* **① `bash scripts/check_bibliography.sh` · ② the theorems ledger in its four parts · ③ the CENSUS PASS — for each result, which of `THE_DISSOLUTION_CENSUS`'s six clusters it enriches, and the landing in that cluster's home.** ***A result that reaches a paper and a ledger but not the census is a result the corpus's own synthesis does not know about.*** **④ the P6 PASS — does any result move a claim from PERMIT toward REQUIRE under `R2`, and if none does, say so.** ***The two passes are complements: the census counts what a result dissolves, P6 weighs whether the credit moved.***

**⌗ ① in detail: `bash scripts/check_bibliography.sh`** *(added r1862).* ***A bake imports named classical results, and each owes a source at the moment it lands.*** *The check resolves every `\cite`, catches duplicate bibitems, and flags any named classical result with no source in the paper that uses it. **Exit 0 is clean.**

***When anything you write touches the substrate, a cut, a layer or a spacetime, you name which one it is, by
its canonical name, from `ONTOLOGY_FOUNDATION_INDEX` §0's ontology table.*** **You pull the ontology in
explicitly. You do not assume it, and you do not leave the reader to reconstruct it from context.**

**The five, in the words to use:**
*① **the substrate** — $\mathrm{dS}_5=SO(5,1)/SO(4,1)$, in flat ambient $M^6$, **the thing that is sliced** ·
② **the background** $\mathrm{dS}_4$ / **a cut** — a four-geometry, **the thing that is reassigned** ·
③ **the exact solutions** — Schwarzschild, Nariai, Friedmann, each a symmetry-broken cut, **matter the bend** ·
④ **the layer** $\mathcal S_t$ — the **three-dimensional existent**, what advances ·
⑤ **spacetime** $(M,g)$ — the **representation**, never an existent.*

***"The substrate" is reserved for ① and nothing else*** — *it is a term of art here, and the only rung word that
is reservable.* **`background`, `geometry` and `ontology` are the field's ordinary words: rung ② is `the
$\mathrm{dS}_4$ background` or `a cut`, qualified every time, and rungs ③–⑤ are named by their phrases.**
**A cut is never called the substrate. The layer is never called spacetime. If a sentence would read the same
with two rungs swapped, it is not yet written.**

## Closing a state advance — four steps, not batchable to end of session
1. **Log `CORPUS_MAP.md`** — a dated `### Revision rNNN` entry in house style: what changed and why.
**⛭ AND WHEN AN ITEM POSES A BINARY, ASK FIRST WHETHER IT IS ONE QUESTION (added r1822, from `A9`).** *Three for three in Lane 3: `A2.2`'s *fixed-by-geometry or rides-on-composition* — **both, on different objects** · `A4.3`'s *geometric or external* — **four answers, not two** · `A4.1`'s *held open* — **bounded, not undetermined**. ***Each dissolved because the SOURCE distinguished two things the item had fused*** — so the check fires **when you open an item**, before working it.*
   **⛭ THEN COUNT THE HEADING — expect exactly one (added r1778).** *`grep -c '^### Revision rNNNN'`. **§12's
   standing warning covers entries LOST to a bad anchor — r1491–r1499 and r1509–r1527, twenty-seven between
   them. r1777 was the opposite: TWO entries under one number**, both written in the same turn, independently
   composed, neither wrong and neither a superset. **A duplicate is quieter than a loss** — the log looks
   fuller, not emptier — *and the fix is not to delete one: **both accounts were kept under one heading at
   r1778**, because a changelog that silently drops a written account is the defect §12's warning exists about.*
2. **Re-run `scripts/depmatrix.py` after ANY citation change** — paste the LaTeX rows into P7's
   `tab:dependency-matrix` **and** refresh `BOOK_INTRO_cosmiCave/assets/dependency_matrix.html` via
   `scripts/gen_matrix_html_tbody.py`.
3. **Cut the bundle with the programme's own tool** — `bash scripts/cut_bundle.sh <rev>`. Never hand-roll a
   `tar`; **never reuse a revision number** — a bundle overwritten is a state destroyed.
4. **Increment.** One revision per state advance, not one per session.

**Why this is a hard rule and not hygiene.** This work is destructive — text deleted, sections rewritten, items
retracted. Each is correct *only if it can be reversed*, and reversibility is a property of **the trail**, not
of the intention. Run without it and a wrong edit made at speed is indistinguishable from a right one, with
nothing to go back to.

## After any significant change to a paper — the masthead check
**⛭ AND FIRST, THE SIMPLER HALF (added r1720): print the section the edit landed in.** *P10's abstract
restates its body closely enough that two placements anchored on body-sounding phrases and **both went into
the abstract**. **A new argument in a précis is a category error** — an abstract summarises what the paper
argues, it does not argue — and "the edit landed" does not tell you **where**.*

**You changed the body. Go read the abstract and the intro**, before the turn closes. A correction lands where
it bites, and a masthead is never where anything bites, so **the summary lags by construction.** Four questions:
1. Does the abstract still describe **this** paper — not *is it true*, but *is it still the paper's shape*?
2. **Do its counts still hold?** "Two further readings" goes silently false when you add a third.
3. Does the intro's roadmap **name the section**?
4. Is the new weight **on** the masthead, or buried in a subordinate clause?

## After any change to a METADOCUMENT — the header-vs-body check *(added r1746; ARC 5 asked for this in its own last line and it was never placed)*
**The masthead check above governs PAPERS. This is the same defect one domain out, and nothing covered it.**
*ARC 5's closing line, written r1486 and unactioned for 260 revisions: "the per-turn list should carry a
header-vs-body check, **since this is the class the programme's own stale-link prior predicts** — *the physics is
built; the pointers between the built things are what rot*."*
**The check, and it is one question: does the heading still describe what is under it?**
1. **A status line** — *"PASS 1, the signs are not yet written"* while the body carries five written signs.
2. **A currency banner** — *"current as of rNNNN"* on a document edited hundreds of revisions later.
3. **A completion claim** — an arc heading calling itself *"the largest owed piece"* **twenty lines above its own
   completion tick**.
4. **A routing instruction** — *"place nothing new here"* on the file everything has been placed in since.
5. **A classification heading** — a `## Retired` section holding a live discriminator that a plan item names.
**⚑ WHY IT IS A PER-TURN CHECK AND NOT A SWEEP: every instance was found by ACCIDENT, while doing something
else, and ARC 5's one deliberate sweep found one of them.** *The seven since: three at **r1697** (the register
stale the same way twice, and the arc heading) · two at **r1742** (the index's 189-revision banner, and the
`## Retired` heading over a live guard) · one at **r1745** (the plan's r1449 routing banner, expired ~290
revisions and sitting above every lane).* **A class still producing instances 260 revisions after its sweep is
standing, not complete** — which is why ARC 5's heading was corrected at r1738 and why this belongs here rather
than in a queue.
**The rule, stated so it costs one line a turn: when you edit a metadocument, read the heading of the section
you edited, and the banner at the document's top.** *Both are claims. Both were true when written. Neither is
checked by anything else.*

## Before editing any paper with a précis abstract — P3 especially
P3's abstract restates the body almost verbatim, so `replace(anchor, …, 1)` **always hits the abstract first**
and the body never gets the edit. **Locate the site by LINE, not by phrase:** `grep -n` the anchor — **two hits
means you are about to edit the wrong one.** Body and abstract carry the claim at different weights and want
different sentences. **Check both after: `abstract=1, body=1`.**

## Two standing guards on CR's own language
- **"Manufactured / shadow / projection / artefact" mean BUILT-BY-CONSTRUCTION-AND-REAL, never *unreal*.**
  Reading them conventionally **inverts the central claim.**
- **The horizon and the centre are topologically identical on the substrate** — two turning points of one
  curve, exchanged by the involution — **and NOT metrically identical**: the curvature is lopsided, finite at
  the horizon, an intrinsic spike at r=0. *"Metrically identical" was retired as false; do not reintroduce it.*

*(Held here until the ontology map is filtered, then onto the cards of the papers they protect — with the
three reading guards: the Hubble/acoustic matter is resolved and reopening it has burned ≥5 collaborators · α
is never sent to a limit · `X` is the throat's size, `r` the signed areal radius.)*

## Writing into the corpus
- **Prior reading earns you the index — *where to look* — never the extract.** Asked what a locus says or
  whether a computation holds: read the locus, re-run it. Do not recite from familiarity.
- **The source rule.** Never assert anything about the corpus — **positive or negative** — that you do not know
  from source. *"The corpus does not claim X" · "this is open" · "this is not built"* are all assertions, and
  the negatives are the ones that slip, because they feel like restraint. Route: **the ontology map card** for
  orientation, then **the abstract-dig at source** for confirmation. The card is a map; the paper is territory.
  **⛭ AND THE FORM TO WRITE INSTEAD — added r1751, Daryl, because the rule said *don't* and never said *what*.**
  *"**Stop writing that things don't exist within the programme that you've never read or looked for.** Why can't
  you make an honest note and resolve it later after it's been honestly logged rather than **fabricating a lie to
  come back and revise later**. That's a far more volatile process that is **not properly aimed at its real
  target** and keeps fucking shit up."*
  **Three parts, all cheap:** **NOT FOUND: `<the thing>`** · **SEARCHED: `<the documents actually opened>`** ·
  **NOT YET CHECKED: `<the documents where it would live if it exists>`.**
  **Why that form and not a softened negative:** *a false negative **creates work to undo** — each of mine had to
  be found, retracted and corrected across three documents — and it **aims at asserting rather than finding
  out**, which is the substitution the driver section forbids. **An honest not-found is resolvable by anyone
  including the next node; a fabricated absence is a landmine that reads as a finding.***
  **Four instances in this arc, three of them bounded searches whose bound went unstated:** *r1699 — the
  r1541–r1563 changelog stretch reported **absent** after checking **two of three entry formats**; all
  twenty-three were present · r1717 — P10's census section called **"mostly removal"** on a keyword scan; it is
  five entries, all CONFIRMED at source · r1746 — a header-vs-body section added while **the bullet already
  existed**, the word counted and its location unread · r1749 — the **closure self-check** written off as
  **"defined nowhere in the programme"** after two documents, **without opening `THE_CLOSURE_LEDGER`, whose name
  is the word**.*
  ***The rule was already here, twice. What was missing was the sentence to write instead of breaking it.***
  **⌗ AND THE NOTE'S HOME IS `CORPUS_MAP`, established by practice rather than by design — resolved r1752.**
  *The r1751 not-found asked where a logged not-found lives. **Checked: `ENTRY_POINT_REGISTER` catalogues gaps the
  corpus advertises in its own words · `THE_WEAVE` is a harvest grid · `OPEN_PROBLEMS_MAP` clusters open problems.
  None holds a searcher-side record**, because a not-found is a fact about the search, not about the corpus.*
  **But the changelog already does, and has for 600 revisions — seven instances before this session, each written
  the honest way:** *r1159 — "the survey was phrase-specific and reported as a finding" · r1324 — an r703
  computation **NOT FOUND** in the bundle, **with the three search terms named** · r1494 — a title search that
  returned nothing because the `\title{}` was line-broken, **run down rather than dropped** · r1518 — "my earlier
  check had searched only for the filenames, which is why it returned nothing" · r1613 — a superseded draft absent
  from the tree, *"recorded in place as dangling rather than quietly dropped"*.*
  **So: log the not-found in `CORPUS_MAP` with its three parts, and the resolution lands there too** — which is
  what r1749→r1750 did. **The changelog is the one grain where a claim and its later correction sit in the same
  record, which is exactly what a not-found needs.** *No new document; the practice was already right and only
  the form was missing.*
- **The corpus publishes as one.** A reader receives all seventeen papers on the same day, so **there is no
  *since*.** Barred: *since built · now built · has since been · is now settled/resolved/built · now a result*.
  Drop the adverb: `has since been built` → `is built`. The test: is the sequence one the **reader** is in, or
  only one the **authors** were in?
- **Route every finding by the destination's purpose.** Does it advance the paper's central objective? It goes
  in the paper, stated as physics. Is it experiential — wiring, not making the same mistake twice? Meta-document,
  never paper. Neither? **Say so and surface it** — it may have no home yet and need one. Do not cram a homeless
  thing into the nearest document because it is nearest. Judge fit **after**, removed from the doing.

## The mirror — ⌗ **the CONTENT of opening-a-turn step 1, not a second check (clarified r1748)**
*The opener above says **run it and make it visible**; this says **how**. They are one act at one moment, and
were written 219 revisions apart — r1483 from the open-problems map's pinned procedure, r1702 from the operating
layer's inert-rule finding. **Read together: step 1 fires it, this supplies the method.***
Before going at the problem, name **the act this turn centres on** — writing a synthesis, receiving evidence,
reaching a verdict, weighing an orchestrator input, reaching past the grounded, a long search that has not
closed — and pull **both poles** of how it fails, from `CODA_FIELD_NOTE.md`. **Most steps can fail either way**
— over-reach *or* under-reach — and a one-sided guard catches half of a balanced step. Go to the field note for
the content; never recite a face from memory. *(From the open-problems map's pinned procedure, r1483. If you
find yourself naming a face from recall rather than reading it, that is the signal to re-read the field note.)*

## Stating something you cannot yet ground
**Highlight it; do not scrub it.** When a reach cannot be firmly grounded, burying it behind "not proven",
hedging it into a caveat or dropping it to a parenthetical is the timid failure — the mirror of manufacturing.
**State the conjecture boldly and tag the proof honestly.** A well-flagged conjecture is a gift to whoever
takes it up; a scrubbed one is lost. *(From the open-problems map's pinned procedure, r1483.)*

## Every turn
- **A hold has THREE stages; check all three (r1588).** ① is the condition met · ② was the release made · ③
  does the document know. **A3.2 was yes/yes/no** — the gate had opened, the material had gone into the corpus,
  and the sketchpad went on holding it. **Checking ① alone reports work owed that is already done.** *All four
  holds discharged in ARC 8 turned out to be doubly stale on this test.*
- **Check a document's header against its body before trusting it** — ⌗ **the full check is its own section
  above, `After any change to a METADOCUMENT` (r1746); this bullet is the r1486 original and is kept for the
  half the section does not carry: it is a check on **reading** as well as on editing.** *A header states what
  was true when someone wrote it; the body states what happened since. **Four stale headers in one session — a
  receipt docstring, a census summary, a map's state banner, a register's status — each of which *understated*
  what had been done.** The programme's own stale-link prior is the reason: the built things hold; the pointers
  between them rot.* **⚠ And this bullet existed when the section was added at r1746 — I counted the word
  "header" in the list and did not read where it was.** *Not a duplication to delete: the two halves are
  **before trusting** and **after editing**, and both are real.*

- **Ongoing work is map-navigated, not full-reread.** Orient off the maps; take weight to the source on every
  load-bearing step.
- **Gap tracking.** Questions raised mid-work get written down as they surface, not left in the turn that
  raised them.

## Closing the turn
- **⛭ THE HANDOFF ITSELF — three things, always, even when nothing is uncertain (Daryl, r1756).** *"You didn't
  leave me with any information about **how you're carrying this forward** or **if there's anything planned** or
  **what you might need from me**. It's the ultimate confusing end of turn handoff."*
  **① WHERE THIS TURN LEFT THE WORK** — *the arc and step by name, what is now closed in it, what is not. Not the
  findings again: the **position**.*
  **② WHAT COMES NEXT AND WHY THAT** — *named, so it can be redirected. **A turn that ends without a next move
  hands the ordering back** — which is the same defect as handing back a decision, one layer up.*
  **③ WHAT IS NEEDED FROM DARYL, INCLUDING "NOTHING"** — *say it explicitly. **Silence on this reads as an
  unstated ask**, and he then has to work out whether he is being waited on.*
  **⚠ AND THE PATTERN THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF, worth naming because it will recur:** *r1751 — the source
  rule forbade unsourced negatives and never said what to write instead. r1753 — the uncertainty rule said when to
  ask and never how. **r1756 — this section says three things not to do and one thing to do, and never said what a
  handoff must contain.** ***The programme's rules are consistently prohibitions without their positive form***,
  because each was written the turn a specific failure happened. **When a rule says only "don't", the positive
  form is missing and someone is paying for it.***
- **⛭ VERIFIED IS NOT DELIVERED (added r1736; it was only in `THE_OPERATING_MANUAL`).** *Cut, copy to the
  outputs directory, **and present**. Six bundles were once cut and each genuinely verified by extracting the
  tarball — **and not one was handed over**, because **a rigorous check of an artifact's contents reads exactly
  like a check that the artifact arrived**. The handover is the last step **of** the work, not a courtesy after
  it: **a result Daryl cannot open is not stated for reversal, it is stated for nobody.***
- **Never write "stated for reversal."** Reversibility is structural; saying it quietly implies the opposite.
- **Do not manufacture a decision point.** Could the source, the logic, or the work settle it? Then it is not
  Daryl's — carry it, and say what you are doing next. Uncertainty earns its place only when it is real, and
  then it is **one clear question**, not a menu.
- **⛭ AND WHEN IT IS REAL, THE SHAPE OF THE HANDOVER — three parts, in this order (Daryl, r1753).** *The rule
  above says **when** to ask and has never said **how**, and the cost of that is his to pay:*
  > *"You spend a turn working and thinking through things and **what I get here is coded half thoughts**, which
  > is fine, but then you come to some uncertainty that ends up **ill-posed because you don't then pause and
  > explain the uncertainty in terms I can understand**. The question shouldn't be just one line or two. It should
  > **introduce itself**, explain or **lay out the context**, then it should **ask the question**. Because the
  > normal way — where I'm just trying to parse thoughts that aren't deliberately placed with context around the
  > question, and uncover what the actual question is with the information required to actually be able to form an
  > opinion — often ends up confusing and is **always pretty opaque and ill-posed**."*
  **① INTRODUCE IT.** *Say a question is coming and what it is about. A question that arrives inside a paragraph
  of findings has to be excavated before it can be answered.*
  **② LAY OUT THE CONTEXT — specifically, the information required to form an opinion.** *Not the reasoning that
  produced the uncertainty; **what someone needs in order to have a view.** The fork's two sides, what each
  costs, what is already established that bears on it, and what I have already ruled out and why.*
  **③ THEN ASK IT.** *One question, after the context, not before it.*
  **⚠ THE FAILURE THIS REPLACES, named so it is recognisable: the rest of the turn is a working record — coded,
  compressed, half-thought — and a question written in that same register inherits its opacity.** *The turn's
  body may be dense; **the question must not be.** Drop out of the working voice to ask.*
  **⌗ And a worked instance of the form, since it is easier to copy than to derive:** *the three-part not-found —
  **NOT FOUND · SEARCHED · NOT YET CHECKED** (r1751) — is this shape applied to one narrow case. It hands over a
  **state with its bounds named**, which can be acted on or ignored, rather than a question that must first be
  reconstructed.*

## The destination — cosmiCave

**The default destination for this work is cosmiCave.** The corpus, its matter sector, its cosmology and cosmogenesis, and the dependency-matrix entry-map all land there — as *The Shadow of Existence* (the book), the essays, and the interactive showpieces (`BOOK_INTRO_cosmiCave/`). Publication (arXiv/CQG) is the parallel formal channel. Unless a specific piece says otherwise, "where does this go" answers **cosmiCave**. *(Standing knowledge, baked r925 — the default a fresh node should assume.)*

## ⌫ Where we are (the r923 state) — **STRATUM, r923. Not the position; see the phase plan at this file's head (r1319, step 2).**

The corpus is 17 papers (P1–P16 + p0/17), complete and self-knowing: the Knows-Itself pass closed the ontology spine both ways across all seventeen, Pass 3 found it complete (zero forced-undrawn edges), and the dependency-matrix artifacts are current (r926) and maintained. **The build work is finished.** What remains sorts into six lanes, below. The shape of it: the corpus is an apparatus built to make a set of decisive, falsifiable claims — and most of what remains is either *waiting for the world to judge those claims*, or *the deep reaches the apparatus opens onto*, or *carrying it to cosmiCave*.

ONE live marker between them.** *The `⌫ STRATUM` marking is doing its job. **The one hit is inside this block:
"P14 PARTIAL — the r1304 whole-comb was a ONE-PASS RUBBER-STAMP, Daryl-caught; the genuine ten-avenue comb is

## ★ STANDING DISCIPLINE (r1279, Daryl) — how we handle open/frontier/problem items, and closures
The corpus's open items, and every move that might close one, are handled through the **closure self-check**
(`THE_CODA.md` → *The closure self-check*; casebook faces *The premature closure* and *The uncertainty-spin*
in `CODA_FIELD_NOTE.md`). The rule, in one line: *is this a closure? → treat-as-yes and explore → unfilled
worked now if it can be, else surfaced-and-handed-over → close only when the corpus supplies all the pieces,
at honestly-earned weight.* Never a close structurally wanted when it was wanted left open. This is the
inward face of the arsenal's **fifth axis** (`THE_ARSENAL.md`), whose two ledgers govern the work: the
`THE_REACH_LEDGER` (claim everything the corpus touches — GR/QM/QFT/SM/gauge/SU(3)/mathematics) and the
`THE_CLOSURE_LEDGER` (find + kill premature closures, in the corpus and in the operation). **These are living
sweeps; nothing in them is ever declared done.** Every list below — the canonical opens, the lanes — is read
through this discipline: an item is closed only when earned, and an apparent close is opened and checked
first.

---

### (historical) the r1241 list

**Single source of truth for what the corpus leaves genuinely open.** It mirrors the corpus's one open
section, **`CR_framework.tex` `sec:frontiers` "Frontiers and open problems"** — the two kept in sync. Standing law
(`THE_OPEN_PROBLEMS_CENSUS.md`): **nothing in the corpus may claim to be open / a frontier / incomplete unless
it is one of these items.** Every other "open / remains / not yet / deferred / unbuilt" claim is a false-open
to be **struck and replaced with the owed reference** to where the result actually is. The ~123 open claims
(r1240 count) collapse onto this handful; that collapse **is** the campaign. Goal: a handful, nothing dangling.

**The candidate opens (from `sec:frontiers` — each is a SUSPECT to be EARNED by the honest whole-corpus check, NOT
inherited as settled-open). The quantum item already fell to that check (r1243); the rest are unchecked and
presumed stale until verified.** `sec:frontiers` states them; the campaign must confirm, for each, that the corpus works
it nowhere before it is allowed to stand as open:
1. **Matter branch-point crossing worldline-and-field dynamics** — crossing is well-posed / isotropizing / scale-free /
   structural-law, first concrete model built (cosmogenesis); the full worldline-and-field dynamics is the
   frontier [dynamics].
2. ~~**The bead's sheet-to-ruling assignment**~~ — **⌫ DISSOLVED, corrected r1840 (Phase 8, probe `Q2`).**
   *This list carried it as "a geometric question, do-not-assert"; **the family was KILLED at r1280 and P7's own
   `§frontiers` now has ZERO mentions of it** — so the list is quoting a paper that no longer says this.*
   ***P7 §two-sided-closure: "they are not two candidates for a single assignment but the linear and antilinear
   faces of the one analytic plate"*** — the rulings borne on `R`'s real $r$-axis, the wings on `K`'s
   $\tilde\tau$-axis. **And one assignment IS derived and stands: the photon — the at-rest congruence reassigned
   to null — rides the real $\mathrm{Im}\,\tilde\tau=0$ crossing** (`photon_cross_test`).
   *(The branch NAMING — antimatter — was already settled and is unaffected.)*
   **⚠ And this list's own instruction was the check that would have caught it: "the campaign must confirm, for
   each, that the corpus works it nowhere before it is allowed to stand as open." It had been worked — in the
   paper the list is quoting.**
3. **The inherited seam-radiation datum ρ_r/ρ_m≈2 (the η-analogue)** — the baryogenesis-analogue derivation;
   may remain empirical without cost to the dissolution — **which does not make the derivation optional**. *(Composition target met — cosmogenesis.)*
4. **The irreducible interior reassignments** — Kerr-inner, RN-interior; gated on the matter-sector dynamics.
   *(The REDUCIBLE sector is fully classified and reached — range paper.)*

   ~8% damping signature) + full-spectrum likelihood vs ΛCDM. *(Large-angle shape computed.)*
6. **A propagating fermion field sector** — the framework's largest unbuilt undertaking, do-not-assert. *(The
   DISCRETE flavour structure is built and forced within CR — P14.)*
7. ~~**The quantum completion (interacting theory)**~~ — **CHECKED r1243: NOT a CR open (stale), MIGRATED
   OFF.** canonical-time §250 closes the quantization ambiguity at *every order of the coupling* by the de
   Sitter horizon's thermal state (not merely the free sector §982 had claimed); the classical nonlinear
   regime is settled (attractor, ghost-free to all orders — dynamics). What remains is the *standard*
   interacting-QFT problem (Γ̂ spectrum, whether bounded below, the UV mode-sums) — the problem every QFT has,
   not a CR gap — and the non-perturbative/nonlinear closed-forms are neither built nor needed. §982 reframed.
- **(standing, empirical) The world-correspondence + the discipline's empirical grounding** — object-level (is CR's fixed point the world's) AND method-level (do the theory-choice rules track truth — the vindication lemma, P6's first programme); one family, two levels (r1254).  — held to the world's verdict on the two tests the
  programme opens (the metric-singularity structural test; the cosmology empirical test, now returning in

## ⌫ CURRENT STATE & ACTIVE THREADS (r1011) — **STRATUM, r1011–r1135. Not the current state; 573 revisions old.**

*(Written r1011 to hold everything the recent sessions have been working, after a long, hard, productive run that ended in a deep notational reckoning. Read this first when picking the work back up.)*

**DONE and bundled this run (r1010–r1011):**
- **The antimatter-progenitor theorem.** `thm:antimatter-progenitor` (P7): our universe issued from an antimatter black hole — the progenitor collapse sits on the conjugate (r<0 = R = antimatter) branch. Baked as conjecture → earned in P7 → promoted to theorem on settled premises → carried across the corpus (P14/P13/P16 reciprocally wired; P3 left pure-geometry by design). Map §1e updated; §1q marked RECONCILED (not a tension). No baryogenesis claim; the matter/antimatter relation is the standing R-conjugation, not a seam event.

7b. **E.1-a · P3's third mass definition — the leg with no formula, no name and no source (opened r1144, WORKED).**
   `SdS-slicing-curve` §mass rests its conclusion (*"the standard definitions return $M$, not $\alpha$"*)
   on **three** definitions. Two now carry formulas **and** doors (Misner--Sharp `\cite{MisnerSharp1964}`,
   Komar `\cite{Komar1959}`, r1144). The third — *"the asymptotically-de~Sitter mass (in place of the
   inapplicable ADM) likewise returns $M$"* — has **none of the three**, and P5's `rem:mass-question`
   imports the same triple. **⌗ The sign (worked r1144, so the next node starts from here):**
   *Where it leads* — **"the asymptotically-de~Sitter mass" is NOT one construction; it is a contested
   class**, and its members **disagree on SdS**: the **Abbott--Deser** charge (Nucl. Phys. B **195** (1982)
   76) evaluated on Schwarzschild--de~Sitter *"gives the mass parameter"* — P3's claim holds — while the
   **conformal / Kastor--Traschen** charge gives *"the mass parameter **times the cosmological scale
   factor**"* — **not $M$**. Others again (Balasubramanian--de~Boer--Minić; Ashtekar--Bonga--Kesavan;
   Anninos--Ng--Strominger; Kelly--Marolf). *Why it matters* — **a citation alone would pick a construction
   on P3's behalf**, which is a physics call, not a gate call: hence this is a **content** item, not the
   citation debt E.1 discharged. *The cheapest next step* — **name which construction P3 means** (if
   Abbott--Deser, say so and cite it; the literature corroborates the SdS evaluation). *The reach, and it
   may be the real prize* — **the literature's own verdict is that a conserved charge in asymptotically-dS
   spacetime is *"generally believed not to be well defined"*** (no spatial infinity; no global timelike
   Killing vector; *"the physical meaning of the Abbott--Deser energy outside the cosmological horizon is
   not clear"*). **That is evidence FOR P3's thesis** — if the asymptotically-dS mass is contested where
   Misner--Sharp and Komar are not, the third leg is an **asset currently stated as a throwaway**, and §mass
   could say so. *Blind spot of the test that found it* — the mechanical gather **cannot see this at all**:
   it names no person, so no catalogue row lists it; **only the paragraph read surfaced it.**
   **✔ CLOSED r1711 — and the item's own "Daryl's call" was the thing under audit, not a fact.** *The gate's question is not which construction Daryl prefers; it is **what P3's argument needs**. Its conclusion — "$\alpha$ is not the gravitational mass; the gravitational mass is the perspectival $M$ the standard definitions measure" — needs a construction that **applies to SdS and returns $M$**, not universal agreement across the class. **Abbott--Deser is one, and the item's own r1144 research already said so.*** **Placed:** *P3 §mass now names it, cites it (`AbbottDeser1982`, bibitem added), and states the third leg at its actual weight — that the class is contested, that conformal/Kastor--Traschen return $M$ times the scale factor, and that a conserved charge in asymptotically-dS spacetime is widely held not well defined.* **And the reach the item flagged as "the real prize" is taken at the weight it earns: the constructions disagree over how to subtract a de Sitter background, which is exactly the step this programme declines — the de Sitter structure being the substrate rather than a background to remove.** *P5's `rem:mass-question`, which imports the same triple, tightened to match.*

## Lane 4 — The reach / unification tests (the vision's decidable edges)

- **⌖ A5.6 · DOES THE SUBSTRATE REACH ELECTROMAGNETIC RADIATION AS A BEND? — placed r1806, and it should have been placed at r1763.**
  ⟐ *material:* P8 §RN–dS · P11 §Gowdy · P13 §rank count · P3's closing section. ⟐ *test:* the numerology-vs-teeth
  discriminator, as for `A5.4`. ⟐
  **THE STATE, established at r1760–r1762 and unchanged since:** *the corpus has **EM stress-energy as a bend of
  the cut** — P8's Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter member, $2m(r)=2M-q^{2}/r$ giving $\rho=q^{2}/8\pi r^{4}=p_t$,
  ***"not an independent posit fed into the geometry; it is the curvature of the cut"*** — and it has
  **gravitational radiation as a bend**, P11's transverse-traceless shear on a leaf. ***What it does not have is
  the one combination: EM RADIATION as a bend.*** And the two bends it does have are **different components** —
  RN–dS's lives in the radial profile, the Gowdy wave's is TT.*
  **⌗ WHY IT IS A LANE 4 ITEM AND NOT A FIGURE ONE:** *it asks whether the substrate **reaches** a sector, which is
  exactly what this lane's tests decide, **and it has a falsifier of the same shape as `A5.1`'s**: if EM radiation
  admits no bend-representation while gravitational radiation does, **the "matter and radiation are the bend"
  anchor is bounded to spin-2 and P11's sentence needs qualifying.***

  **⚑ ✔ RUN r1831 — AND THE ITEM'S FRAMING WAS WRONG. The gap compared across the wall.**
  ***The obstruction is not P13's rank count. It is `prop:radiative`, and it excludes free EM and free
  GRAVITATIONAL radiation by one mechanism:*** *"the reachable vacuum kernels are the non-radiative algebraic
  types — O, D, I; **the purely radiative types N and III do not occur**," because* ***"a swept geometry depends
  only on its ORBIT-SPACE coordinates while a free wave depends on the TRANSVERSE coordinates through which it
  propagates."***
  **A null EM beam is $T^{\mu\nu}\propto k^{\mu}k^{\nu}$ — traceless, anisotropic, and dependent on the transverse
  coordinates it propagates through. A sweep cannot carry it, for the identical reason it cannot carry a free
  graviton.**
  **⌗ SO THE THREE-TERM COMPARISON THAT DEFINED THE GAP WAS MIS-MATCHED:** *P8's RN–dS EM is a **STATIC** Coulomb
  field, in the range · P11's Gowdy is a **CONFINED** wave — *"type I with two Killing vectors, hence in the
  range"*, its propagation **pinned by a residual isometry** · and **free gravitational radiation is OUTSIDE the
  generative sector**, past the wall.* ***So EM was being compared one rung low: a static field against a confined
  wave, and the conclusion drawn was that EM lags by a sector. It does not.***
  **⛭ THE SYMMETRIC STATEMENT, which is the item's actual answer:** *EM radiation sits **exactly where
  gravitational radiation sits** — **outside the generative reach when free** (both excluded by `prop:radiative`),
  and **inside it when confined by a residual isometry.*** **And the range theorem is a condition on the
  GEOMETRY's isometry group, not on the matter**: *"a geometry is a cut when its isometry group contains a
  sweep-subgroup of $\mathfrak{so}(5,1)$… and matter is the bend."* *`rem:charge` already runs the electrovac case
  and finds the Einstein–Maxwell–$\Lambda$ equations hold identically with charge as the bend, rotation-independently
  through Kerr–Newman–(A)dS.*
  ***So a confined EM wave — the Einstein–Maxwell analogue of the Gowdy member, two Killing vectors — is indicated
  to be in the range by the range theorem itself, since the theorem conditions on the geometry's symmetry and the
  charged members are already electrovac cuts.*** **⚠ INDICATED, NOT COMPUTED — that is the honest weight, and it
  is the one piece of A5.6 that remains: exhibit one, or find the obstruction.**
  **⌗ AND WHAT THIS DOES TO P11's ANCHOR: nothing. *"Matter and radiation are the bend"* stands unqualified** —
  *the bend carries radiation exactly as far as the sweep reaches, for spin-1 and spin-2 alike, and hands off at
  the same wall.* **The falsifier this item carried — *"if EM admits no bend-representation while gravitational
  radiation does, the anchor is bounded to spin-2"* — is answered: the antecedent is false, because gravitational

- **D.13 · THE FORWARD POINTER — replace defensive negations with where the work IS (opened r1583, Daryl).**
  **The pattern, in Daryl's words:** *"There's a thing my wife really hates: drunk guys who have to tell you
  all about how they're a really good guy… **Why not introduce the thing positively as rigorously taken up in
  the sequel?** … Don't gesture at him as the drunk guy in the corner he is right now, not bothering a soul.
  **Gesture forward to him as the cat rescuer he will be.**"*
  **Why the negation form is the defect:** it must **invoke the dismissive reading in order to deny it**, and
  it still never says where the work is. *"Not a technicality deferred to the next paper"* leaves the reader
  holding the words *technicality* and *deferred*. **The forward pointer does neither job badly: it names where
  the thing is taken up, and it never raises the bad framing at all.**
  **And it closes the arc.** *"We could even go back and say, at the place this thing is raised, that it is a
  matter for that thing and it's taken up and closed in this way over there."* **Two pointers, one arc: raised
  here, carried there, closed.**
  **⚑ First instance done r1583 — P2.** *"What that leaves is **not a technicality deferred to the next paper**
  but the sequence's subject"* → *"What that leaves is **the sequence's subject, taken up and carried through
  in the companions**."* P2 recompiles clean, 26pp.
  **The distinction that keeps this from over-firing:** *"not merely X but Y"* asserting something **stronger**
  is legitimate and stays — *"empirically favoured, not merely more economical"* is a real claim. **The target
  is only the construction that denies being unimportant.**
  ⟐ *scope:* the corpus, then the metadocs · *runs as:* **SA-3**, a standing action — the instances are found by
  reading, not searching · *feeds:* every paper's cross-references, and the closed-arc structure.

  **⚑ AND THE SWEEP'S BEST FINDING WAS AVAILABLE BY THAT ROUTE, NOW: P10 did not know it.** *P10 owns the
  $\hbar$-at-the-seam result — the Hartle–Hawking state at $\kappa=1/\alpha$ selecting the Friedrichs
  extension — and carried **zero** mentions of *two real forms*, *Wick*, or $S^{5}$, while p0 says **that very
  continuation is the substrate's other real form**. **Placed r1719:** the extension is fixed not by an extra

  **What remains genuinely gated is smaller and better defined:** *whatever the geometry proofs **add**. The
  constants route is open now and has one paper's worth of yield already; papers still carrying none of it —
  P1, P4, P6, P15 — to be judged on what each owns, never on the keyword.* ⟐ *depends on:* **A2.9** (the turnaround
  cubic's positive geometry), **A2.10 §II.3** (the 2/3 exponent's three jobs), and the geometry ledger's
  programme · *material:* `THE_GEOMETRY_AND_THE_PHYSICS.md`, `FIGURE_THEOREM_LEDGER.md` · *feeds:* the driver's

- **D.10 · WORK OR CUT EVERY `[reach]` — PB-2's clearance condition (entered r1570, CORRECTED r1571).**
  **Not "vet".** ***Every `[reach]` in the corpus is either worked to a result or cut.*** A reach with a stated
  route and a falsifier **is still unworked** — a falsifier describes it, it does not settle it. **Nothing is
  retained as a tagged suspicion, however well-tagged.**
  **Scope — ⚠ RE-COUNTED r1724, and it has GROWN since r1570:** **17** `[reach]` (still all p0, was 15) · `do-not-assert` **×43 across 14 files** (was ×37 across 11) · *"at weight"* ×7 (unchanged). *A clearance condition whose scope is quoted from 154 revisions ago will under-state the job; **the tags are working instruments and the corpus has been adding them**, which is correct behaviour and exactly why the count must be taken at run time rather than carried.*
  **And the de-jargoning is not a separate step — it is what *worked or cut* produces:** a worked reach is
  stated as a result in ordinary scientific prose and the tag is gone; a cut reach is gone. *(`D.11` withdrawn
  and folded in here r1571.)*
  ⟐ *test:* `GEOMETRY_PHYSICS_TAXONOMY`'s numerology-vs-teeth discriminator — **which is the working test, not
  a vetting test: teeth means a mechanism that forces the result, and that is what "worked" requires** ·
  *material:* `JARGON_LEDGER.md` · *feeds:* p0 above all, and every file carrying `do-not-assert`.
  **⛔ TIMING: this is a LATER polishing exercise (Daryl, r1573).** The tags stay in place and keep doing their
  drafting job until the work they organise is finished. **It blocks publication; it does not block the work
  before publication** — and everything before it in the docket blocks publication too.

   r1624.** The unification this row records is precisely what made it false: **one bibkey per paper is now
   correct**, and the map was still telling readers the opposite. Re-verified exhaustively at r1624 — all six
   aliases **zero uses, zero `\bibitem` definitions**, seventeen canonical keys, `depmatrix.py` matching them
   exactly. The map is now the *record* of the defect plus the one rule that outlives it (*absence in a grep

   warning standing for 58 revisions, in a document the index told nodes to read first.*

**⚑ AND THE QUESTION IS ANSWERABLE NOW, which it was not when written.** *P14 **is** the standalone paper —
58 KB, seven sections — and it **draws its own boundary explicitly**: delivered are the generation count, the

ordinary route, a bundle imposed by hand"*) and the mass spectrum (*"the zero-modes are massless, and their

claims.*** **What a spin-off would carry is therefore not a re-cut of P14 but open family 6 — the propagating
fermion sector**, where the ledger's honest state is that `B2_zeromode_continuation.py` **computes** the
bound→propagating transition on one mode of one model and **asserts without computing** the $a^{-3/2}$
cosmological-Dirac form, the $\gamma^5$-preservation, and the three-family continuation. **⌗ Re-scoped: this is
not a shape question about an existing paper; it is "does family 6, when built, want its own paper?" — which
cannot be answered before the build and should not be carried as though it can.** *Gated on family 6, and said
so.*

## Lane 8 — The combinatorics ledger, and the cascade from one gauge (opened r1138, Daryl-directed) — **✔ COMPLETE (r1157)**
**⌖ VERIFIED r1783 (ARC 11) — the completion holds, and one thing was owed.**
**⌗ AND ITS SUB-ITEMS ARE CLOSED WITH IT — marked r1829 so a reader can see it at the entry.** *`L8.0` the discriminator **built** · `L8.1` the number audit **CLOSED r1155** (6 welds · 9 proved splits · 2 dependent · 1 undecided — ***it did not return "it's all one"***) · `L8.2` the sorting **r1156** · `L8.3` the marriage **r1157** (on-vs-tangent to the one circle). **Result baked at p0 §unification's seventh face and map §1u.** ***None of the four is a live docket item, and a code-scan that does not read the lane heading will report all four as open.***
*Checked at source rather than from the claim: **the bake is real** — p0 states the seventh face and names it *"a second way of seeing the object and not as evidence for it; **a reading that returned new physics from a theorem about circles would be an object of suspicion, and one that returns what was already proved, from unrelated directions, is doing what a faithful dictionary does**"* — and **P14 §sec:twofactors is the citable home**, reaching the same factorisation *"from the numerals alone, and **without being told to look**"*, with the law: **the numbers weld WITHIN a factor and split ACROSS it.***
**⚠ AND THE THING OWED, the r1757 pattern again: NINE L8 receipts exist and only THREE were registered.** *The three P14 cites are in `receipts/P14_matter_sector_paper/`; **the other six live in `storyboard_receipts/`, are cited by `COMBINATORICS_LEDGER`, and had zero entries in `receipts/INDEX.md`.** All six **re-run r1783 and pass**. **Registered.** *A receipt cited by a ledger is not a receipt in the registry — second instance, after U1 at r1757.***
**⌗ And the six are not filler; they are the audit's negative half, which is what gives the law its teeth:** *two **DEPENDENT** verdicts, one **resonance-not-identity**, one numeral coincidence caught (*the arc's 120:240 is a **length ratio** wearing a 3's clothes*), **one receipt that caught §0 contradicting itself**, and the tally: ***independent YES 6, NO 9, DEPENDENT 2, UNDECIDED 1 — "the audit did not return 'it's all one'."***
*This section is the lane's plan, kept as the record of the work. The lane ran to completion at r1157: L8.0 discriminator built (r1145), L8.1 audit closed (r1155), L8.2 the sorting (r1156), L8.3 the marriage (r1157); the publication blocker is discharged and the result is baked (p0 §unification's seventh face; map §1u). The full worked ledger is `COMBINATORICS_LEDGER.md`.*

*Sibling of Lane 7 and built the same way: Lane 7 asked **what would Euclid do with this structure**; this
asks **what would a root-system combinatorist do with it**. Same method — run the catalogue against the
object one theorem at a time, keep the failures, let the law be **found** rather than posited.*

**⚠ THE FINDING THAT OPENS IT (r1138, dug at source).** The corpus is **saturated** with combinatorics and
**never names it**: `A₂` in **12 papers / 96 hits** · `S₃` **9 / 101** · `Weyl` **12 / 94** · `cubic` **13 /
145** · `D₆` **8 / 42** · `deck` **8 / 36** · `triple-angle` **5 / 27** · `hexad` **5 / 25** · `monodromy`
**3 / 25**. The word *"combinatorial"* appears **once, in one paper** — P5 §466 — **and it is a guard
against the reading**: *"the ℤ₂ is **not merely a combinatorial automorphism** of the root system **but**
the substrate's own orientation parity."* **The corpus has the guard and has never made the positive
statement.** Daryl, r1138: *"a perfect merging of geometry and combinatorics, the entire structure falling
from one gauge that breaks symmetries in a cascading way… geometry, optics, and combinatorics algebras with
that absolute foliation."*

- **L8.1 · THE NUMBER AUDIT — which of the corpus's numbers are one number?** The instrument exists and has
  a **worked instance and a worked negative**, which is what earned Lane 7 its law. *Instance* (§0, r1103):
  **four of the corpus's numbers are ONE factorisation** — the seam `α/√3`, the backward root `−2α/√3`, the
  turnaround `∛2·α/√3`, and the **120:240 arc split**, all read off `(r−ρ)²(r+2ρ)`; *"Nariai is not an extra
  input — it is what the double root **means**."* *Negative, same entry:* the lift's 1:2 is **not** that 2 —
  *"two 2s, not one and not three"*, `lem:twoturnings`, two affinely inequivalent cubics with no canonical
  identification. **The audit runs that over the whole set:** 3 (roots · hinges · walls · generations) · 6
  (Nariai · hinges both horns) · 12 (designations · |D₆|) · 2 (chiralities · horns · rulings · the double
  root's) · 120/240 · 30/60 · √3 · ∛2 · 2α · the 2:1 lift. **Each pair: one number, or two that agree?**
  Every merge is a result; every refusal is the law getting finer.
- **L8.2 · THE CASCADE, as a cascade.** The chain is in the corpus (`§1t`; p0 §unification's seventh face,
  r1134): `α` → the hole → the Thales circle → `2α` (**output**) → `sin w=½` → `sin 3w=1` → **Nariai** →
  the twelve designations → the hexad → `A₂` → `D₆` → three walls → **3 generations × 2 chiralities**.
  *"Fourteen objects, one input, and the input is the gauge. No principle anywhere."* What is **not** drawn
  is that chain read as a **symmetry-breaking cascade** — each step the next symmetry the gauge breaks —
  which is the reading Daryl names and which p0's *"physics as broken-symmetry shadows"* is already the
  frame for.
- **L8.3 · The marriage, stated once.** Geometry ∧ optics ∧ combinatorics ∧ the absolute foliation. The
  corpus does all four and names the join nowhere. **Where it would land is the open question** — p0's
  §unification is the only synthesis pole, and it now has the one-circle rung this would sit beside.
- **⛔ THE GUARD, and it is the corpus's own and it is not optional.** P5's single mention exists to stop
  the A₂ being read as *merely* combinatorial — the move that read it as **colour** (`rem:a2-distinct`, the
  drill-site held open at r695). **Naming the combinatorics must not undo the guard.** The ledger's law must
  separate, per site: a **structural** identity (the geometry produces it) from a **shared abstract type**
  (the same root system, different realisation) — which is exactly the open question `rem:a2-distinct`
  refuses to close in either direction. **A ledger that returns "it's all one" has rigged its question**
  (`THE_CODA` §"The source answers back"); the failures are what make it a law.
- **Deliverable:** `COMBINATORICS_LEDGER.md` + `storyboard_receipts/`, on Lane 7's pattern — statements,
  receipts that can return NO, and the deaths that cut the law. **✔ OPENED r1145 — §L8.0, THE
  DISCRIMINATOR, IS BUILT; L8.1–L8.3 NOT RUN.** *(The gate's first deliverable was the rule, per this
  lane's own condition, not a merge.)* **It is calibrated on the corpus's own worked YES and worked NO,
  not invented:** the double root's four numbers = one factorisation (§0 r1103) against
  `lem:twoturnings`'s two 2s. **The rule:** *a structural identity requires a **derivation** — one object
  producing both with no new input; a shared abstract type is established by a **proof of inequivalence**;
  neither ⟹ **UNDECIDED**, and undecided is honest.* **The asymmetry that makes it a law: a NO must be
  EARNED** — `lem:twoturnings` **proves** affine inequivalence, it does not report a failed search; *"no
  link found"* is UNDECIDED, and reading it as "two numbers" rigs the question sceptically exactly as
  "it's all one" rigs it credulously. **★ And the indexing rule, which is the sharpest piece and is the
  corpus's own: the verdict belongs to the CLAIM, not the pair.** The same two turnings return **NO** to
  *"the two ℤ₃'s are one symmetry"* (proved) and **OPEN** to *"there is a metric ratio between the two
  radii"* (`A = ∛2·ρ`, named and unrun) — *simultaneously, both honest*. ⟹ **every entry states the claim
  it adjudicates; a pair-indexed entry is malformed**, and a collapsed claim can only be answered one way.
  **Applied to `rem:a2-distinct` it separates three things a careless ledger sees as one:** shared
  abstract type (**YES**), realised colour gauge (**NO**, `su(3)⊄so(5,1)`), structural reason for the
  shared type (**OPEN**) — **and row 2 does NOT settle row 3**, which is the trap, and the mirror of the
  r695 colour read. **Next: L8.1's audit, one pair per sitting, each with its claim written first.**

## ⌖ THE TWO ARCS AS THE ORDERING LENS FOR THE REACH SWEEPS (opened r1741, Daryl)
**Daryl's mechanism, and it inverts how the reach items have been approached:** *"this gives us a mechanism or
lens through which to run the physics and mathematics fishing sweeps more meaningfully. For ordering them…
**by drawing and enriching each arc within the introduction's own framing, taking the overlooked papers in the
corpus on the first round of drafting the physics arc as a guide**, we could develop a more meaningfully ordered
plan that continually develops as we work. **With the top level introduction summary arc's own development held
as a guide to do the work.**"*
**What this replaces.** *`THE_MATHEMATICS_REACH` (the R-M theater — *throw a whole field at the architecture,
keep what bites, the bounce-off is data*) and `THE_PHYSICS_REACH` (R-P) are material for **items 7 and 8, the
two bakes**, and they have had **no internal order** — a fishing sweep with no priority is a sweep that fishes
wherever the net happens to land.*
**⛭ THE LENS: `INTRODUCTION` §2 now carries two arcs, and each is an ORDERED list of what the corpus's deepest
holders would find most arresting. That order IS the sweep order.**
- **The physicist's arc (ten stations, r1740/r1741):** *P12 · P10 · **P8** · P9 · **P11** · P13→P14 · p0 ·
  P3+**P5**+P2 · P7→P15→P16 · P1+P4.* **→ orders the R-P sweep.**
- **The mathematician's arc (eight stations, r1741):** *Euclid/power-of-a-point · the forced triple angle ·
  the continuation and its monodromy · the family's Galois structure · root systems and the on-versus-tangent
  factorisation · two real forms of $SO(6,\mathbb C)$ · Lie algebroids · the graded index.* **→ orders the R-M
  sweep.**
**⚑ AND THE FIRST ROUND OF DRAFTING ALREADY PRODUCED A FINDING, which is why this works.** *The physics arc's
first draft (r1740) **omitted P5, P8 and P11** — and **P8 is the paper the entire mass reading rests on**
("the offset is the mass"), P11 owns the bend's dynamics and the GW-energy dissolution, P5 holds the
shadow-reading in closed form. **The omission was the diagnostic: the papers an arc forgets are the papers whose
implications have not been drawn out.** Woven in at r1741, and each brought a result the arc had been leaning on
without naming.*
**⌗ HOW IT RUNS, and it is a by-product rule rather than a campaign:** *when a sweep or a read produces a result,
ask **where in the two arcs it belongs** — and if it belongs nowhere, that is either a new station or a sign the
result is thinner than it looked. **The arcs are enriched as the work runs, and their state is the plan's own
readout of what the corpus can currently say to its deepest readers.***

## ⌗ THE FIGURE IDEAS REGISTER — a holding list, deliberately NOT a work queue (opened r1726, Daryl-directed)
**Why it is separate, in Daryl's terms:** *"a good one is worth a thousand words. **But the concern attached to it
all is that figure builds, if not approached in the right way, are rabbit holes that can really derail progress.**
I don't want to clean up any dangling figure build possibilities as we audit. I want them organised within the
plan and linked to their standing physics plan items, but separated out into a separate list that is approached
deliberately later."*
**⛭ DARYL'S DRAWING CONVENTIONS — they govern EVERY entry here, and the record says they were "repeatedly
overridden" (lifted from Lane 7 `F.1` at r1763).**
> **`colour = WHOSE it is`** — purple / red / blue = the three hinges.
> **`style = WHERE it is`** — solid = hinge to first hit · dotted = onward · dashed = past the pole.
> ***"Build slowly, one change at a time, his conventions, no recolouring."***
*They were inside one lane item, where only a node working that item would meet them. **They are a constraint on
all figure work, so they sit at the register's head.***

**⛔ THE GOVERNING RULE, and it is the whole point of the separation: NOTHING HERE IS PICKED UP OPPORTUNISTICALLY.**
*A figure idea met during other work is **written here and left**. Building one is a deliberate act taken on its
own turn, never a detour from a physics item — and the r1110 arc is the evidence: it produced a
thirty-one-lemma chain **and** two documented errors, both of which lived in the drawing.*
**⚑ AND THE ORIENTING FINDING, r1725: a figure ENRICHES what is already argued; it does not carry the argument.**
*p0's `sec:unification` way 6 argues this lane's whole chain in prose, without the picture. So every entry here is
scoped as **illustration of a standing result**, and an entry that seems to be *needed* for an argument is a
signal the argument is not yet written.*

  horizon (three independent routes); the tangent from any point is null; the nine-point circle IS the
  throat (a sixth route to 2α). What died: Ptolemy, La Hire, Casey — each death cut the law finer.*
